and define its reward by evaluating the full transcript:

|     |                                                                                                                                            |     |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- |
|     | $`\displaystyle r(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}},\mathbf{z})\;:=\;r\!\bigl(\mathbf{x},\,(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}\circ\mathbf{z}\bigr).`$ |     |

The PrefixRL objective is

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |     |       |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{pre}}(\pi)\;=\;\sum_{\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}\sim\pi(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}},\mathbf{z})\bigr]\;+\;\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\pi(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\bigr].`$ |     | (C.3) |

A uniform upper bound. Fix any $`\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}`$ and any
prefix $`(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}`$ used to form a prefixed
problem. For any policy $`\pi\in\Pi`$ defined on such prefixed problems,
construct a policy $`\tilde{\pi}\in\Pi`$ for the no-prefix problem
$`\mathbf{x}`$ that deterministically emits the prefix
$`(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}`$ and then samples the suffix from
$`\pi(\cdot\mid\mathrm{concat}(\mathbf{x},(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}))`$.
By the reward definition,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |     |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\tilde{\pi}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\bigr]\;=\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}\sim\pi(\cdot\mid\mathrm{concat}(\mathbf{x},(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}))}\bigl[r(\mathrm{concat}(\mathbf{x},(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}),\mathbf{z})\bigr].`$ |     |

Therefore, for every $`\pi`$ and every such prefixed problem
$`\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}}=\mathrm{concat}(\mathbf{x},(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h})`$,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |     |       |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}\sim\pi(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}},\mathbf{z})\bigr]\;\leq\;\max_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\bigr].`$ |     | (C.4) |

Let $`m(\mathbf{x})`$ be the number of prefixed problems in
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}`$ derived from $`\mathbf{x}`$. Summing the
above inequality over all prefixed problems and grouping by their
originating $`\mathbf{x}`$ gives

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |     |       |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}},\cdot)\bigr]\;\leq\;\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}m(\mathbf{x})\,\max_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\bigr].`$ |     | (C.5) |

Define the constant

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                   |     |       |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle C\;:=\;\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}m(\mathbf{x})\,\max_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\bigr],`$ |     | (C.6) |

which is independent of $`\pi`$. Then for every $`\pi\in\Pi`$ we have
the uniform upper bound

|     |                                                          |     |       |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{pre}}(\pi)\;\leq\;C+J(\pi).`$ |     | (C.7) |

Tightness using
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Realizability and correctness of off-policy traces). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes").
By realizability in
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Realizability and correctness of off-policy traces). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"),
there exists a policy $`\mu\in\Pi`$ such that

|     |                                                                                                      |     |       |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mu(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}}\mid\mathbf{x})=1,\qquad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}.`$ |     | (C.8) |

In particular, for any cut $`h`$, conditioning $`\mu`$ on the prefix
$`(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h}`$ yields the deterministic
continuation:

|     |                                                                                                                                                                      |                         |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | ------ | --- | ----- |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mu\!\bigl(\mathbf{z}\mid\mathrm{concat}(\mathbf{x},(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h})\bigr)\;=\;\mathbf{1}\{\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{h+1: | \mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}} | }\}.`$ |     | (C.9) |

Therefore, on every prefixed problem
$`\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}}=\mathrm{concat}(\mathbf{x},(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})_{1:h})`$,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                     |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}\sim\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}},\mathbf{z})\bigr]\;=\;r\!\bigl(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}}\bigr)\;=\;1,`$ |     | (C.10) |

where the last equality uses correctness of
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ in
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Realizability and correctness of off-policy traces). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes").
Hence,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |     |        |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}\sim\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{pre}},\mathbf{z})\bigr]\;=\;\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}m(\mathbf{x}).`$ |     | (C.11) |

Moreover, by
([C.8](#A3.E8 "Equation C.8 ‣ C.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
and correctness,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |             |     |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)\;=\;\sum*{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}*{\mathbf{y}\sim\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}\bigl[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\bigr]\;=\;\sum\_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})\;=\; | \mathcal{D} | .`$ |     | (C.12) |

Therefore $`J^{\star}=|\mathcal{D}|`$ and $`J(\mu)=J^{\star}`$. Since
rewards are in $`[0,1]`$, for each $`\mathbf{x}`$ we have
$`\max_{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\pi^{\prime}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{x})}[r(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})]=1`$,
and thus the constant simplifies to
$`C=\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}m(\mathbf{x})`$. Combining with
([C.11](#A3.E11 "Equation C.11 ‣ C.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
yields

|     |                                                                       |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J_{\mathrm{pre}}(\mu)\;=\;C+J(\mu)\;=\;C+J^{\star}.`$ |     | (C.13) |

Step 3: Concluding the consistency of PrefixRL objective. Let
$`\hat{\pi}\in\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}J_{\mathrm{pre}}(\pi)`$ be any
maximizer of the PrefixRL objective. By optimality of $`\hat{\pi}`$,
([C.13](#A3.E13 "Equation C.13 ‣ C.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
and the upper bound
([C.7](#A3.E7 "Equation C.7 ‣ C.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),

|     |                                                                                                                   |     |        |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle C+J(\hat{\pi})\;\geq\;J_{\mathrm{pre}}(\hat{\pi})\;\geq\;J_{\mathrm{pre}}(\mu)\;=\;C+J^{\star}.`$ |     | (C.14) |

Cancelling $`C`$ yields $`J(\hat{\pi})\geq J^{\star}`$, hence
$`J(\hat{\pi})=J^{\star}`$. Therefore
$`\hat{\pi}\in\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}J(\pi)`$, proving that any maximizer
of the PrefixRL objective also maximizes the standard RL objective.

### C.2 Proof of Theorem [3.3](#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3.3 (Suboptimality gap of PrefixRL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")

In this section we present our proof for
Theorem [3.3](#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3.3 (Suboptimality gap of PrefixRL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
which bounds the performance suboptimality for PrefixRL. In particaly,
we bound thisgap for an algorithm that conforms to the PrefixRL workflow
(Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
but uses natural policy gradient (NPG) \[[Kakade, 2001](#bib.bib33)\] to
update the policy iteratively (starting from the base LLM $`\pi^{0}`$).
In our practical implementation use REINFORCE to compute on-policy
gradients. Next we introduce the setup and describe the key steps in
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"),
some differences with practice and the full proof.

Setup. We use $`\pi^{0}`$ to denote the base LLM we start RL training
with, and $`\mu`$ as the policy used to IID sample the dataset of
off-policy traces $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, one trace for each
problem in $`\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}`$. We assume that
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ is realizable
(Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Realizability and correctness of off-policy traces). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
which implies that it only consists of correct off-policy traces. Let
$`\mathcal{F}`$ denote the class of $`Q`$-functions induced by all
policies in the policy class $`\Pi`$, and $`H`$ be the maximum context
length or horizon $`H`$ of the auto-regressive Markov decision process
(MDP) induced by the policies in $`\Pi`$ and reward function $`r`$.

Description of PrefixRL with NPG
(Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")).
In each iteration of
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
we _first_ collect a dataset of state, action and reward triplets
$`\mathcal{D}_{t}`$. _Second_, we fit a critic or $`Q`$ function
$`\hat{Q}^{t}`$ on this dataset of size $`N`$ (step 9). _Third_, we use
the fitted $`Q`$ function to perform a state-wise mirror ascent or
natural policy update (step 11) in order to get the subsequent RL
iterate. We collect the $`N`$ traces in $`\mathcal{D}_{t}`$ by uniformly
sampling an off-policy state $`\mathbf{s}_{h}`$ (prefixed problem) from
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. Then, we rollout the current RL policy
$`\pi^{t}`$ conditioned on state $`\mathbf{s}_{h}`$ to sample a single
action or token $`a_{h}`$ (step 6). To estimate the $`Q`$ function under
the current RL policy at this state-action pair we now complete the
rollout till time step $`H`$ and collect a reward (step 7).

1: Base policy $`\pi^{0}`$, off-policy data
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, horizon $`H`$, iterations $`T`$, step
size $`\eta`$, $`Q`$ function class $`\mathcal{F}`$.

2: Initialize the iterative algorithm with base policy:
$`\pi^{1}\leftarrow\pi^{0}`$.

3: for $`t=1,\dots,T`$ do

4:   Initialize dataset $`\mathcal{D}_{t}\leftarrow\{\}`$.

5:   for $`i=1\ldots n`$ do

6:    Sample $`(\mathbf{s}_{h},a_{h}^{\mathrm{off}})`$ uniformly across
state-action pairs in $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. $`\triangleright`$
sample prefixed problem

7:    $`a_{h}\leftarrow a_{h}^{\mathrm{off}}`$ with probability
$`\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}`$ and $`\sim\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{h})`$
otherwise.

8:    Execute $`\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{h},a_{h})`$ from step
$`h{+}1`$ through $`H`$ to obtain the full trace with reward $`r`$.

9:
   $`\mathcal{D}_{t}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{t}\cup(\mathbf{s}_{h},a_{h},r)`$.

10:    Critic fit (regression oracle):

11:       
$`\hat{Q}^{t}\leftarrow\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{(\mathbf{s},a,r)\in\mathcal{D}_{t}}(f(\mathbf{s},a)-r)^{2}`$.

12:    Natural policy update (mirror ascent): $`\triangleright`$
performed state-wise

13:       
$`\pi^{t+1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\leftarrow\arg\min_{p}\ \langle-\hat{Q}^{t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),p\rangle+\tfrac{1}{\eta}{\mathrm{KL}}\left(p\,\|\,\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\right)`$.

14:   end for

15: end for

16: return $`\bar{\pi}_{T}\leftarrow\tfrac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi^{t}`$.
$`\triangleright`$ return mixture policy

Algorithm 1 PrefixRL with Natural Policy Gradients

Difference with practice:
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
uses $`Q`$ functions instead of direct rewards. The update in NPG is
similar to REINFORCE except that we use $`N`$ on-policy samples to first
estimate a $`Q`$ function (step 10) in the $`Q`$ function class
$`\mathcal{F}`$) for the current RL iterate $`\pi^{t}`$ and then use the
estimated $`Q`$ function to update the policy and get $`\pi_{t+1}`$
using mirror ascent (step 12). This is a bit different from REINFORCE
where we compute the policy gradient using only the rewards attaind by
the $`N`$ on-policy traces and perform a step of gradient.

Difference with practice:
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
samples new prefixed problems from $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. In
practice we construct a prefixed problems from a fixed dataset of
off-policy traces $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ and also use the set of
prefixed problems in $`\cal{D}_{\mathrm{pre}}`$ are fixed throughout RL
training. In contrast,
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
samples off-policy states (prefixed problems) from the dataset of
off-policy traces $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. This difference is
pretty minor but perhaps underscores the performance improvements driven
by back-generalization in being able to improve performance on the
original no-prefix problems despite PrefixRL only using a small fraction
of all possible off-policy prefixes in $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$.

Comparison with [Chang et al. \[2024\]](#bib.bib10). Our proof technique
follows [Chang et al. \[2024\]](#bib.bib10), adapting to the setting of
verifiable rewards with our different “reset” policy (which we refer to
as prefix policy $`\mu`$), and removing the requirement of KL divergence
between the current and the reset policy. Since our off-policy dataset
consists of only realizable correct traces we will need much weaker
assumptions. Following are some key differences compared to Algorithm 3
in [Chang et al. \[2024\]](#bib.bib10) that allows us to prove the
suboptimality gap with weaker assumptions. _First_, we sample the prefix
from the comparator policy (in other words the prefix generating policy
is realizable and lies in the class of optimal policies). This ensures
sufficient coverage for the distribution of Q-function regression
(ensuring small error in fitting the critic) over states visited by a
“good” policy even though the current RL iterate is far from it.
_Second_, we output the mixture policy (standard in self-play
literature \[[Bai et al., 2020](#bib.bib8), [Hofbauer and Sorin,
2006](#bib.bib29)\]). _Finally_, unlike [Chang et al.
\[2024\]](#bib.bib10), we don’t require a bound on the KL divergence
against the SFT policy or the policy trained on the off-policy data.

Assumptions needed for
Theorem [3.3](#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3.3 (Suboptimality gap of PrefixRL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes").
Now, we list the assumptions we make in our analysis of the
suboptimality gap of PrefixRL. In general, they are milder than the
assumptions in [Chang et al. \[2024\]](#bib.bib10).

- •
  Assumption [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption C.1 (Realizability of 𝑄-function class). ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
  is pretty standard in the analysis of actor-critic methods \[[Haarnoja
  et al., 2018](#bib.bib27)\] and only requires that our critic function
  class is expressive enough to realize the $`Q`$ function induced by
  any policy in $`\Pi`$. Note that since rewards are binary and terminal
  the $`Q`$-value at any state $`\in[0,1]`$.
- •
  Assumption [C.2](#A3.Thmtheorem2 "Assumption C.2 (Correctness and realizability of 𝒟_off). ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
  is a milder form of the typical assumption on the coverage over states
  visited by the optimal policy. Here, we only assume that there is an
  optimal policy that can fit the dataset $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$
  we collected. Typically the coverage assumption places a uniform bound
  on the likelihood ratio over the state distributions of the optimal
  policy and the current RL policy $`(d^{\pi^{\star}}/d^{\pi})`$ as in
  [Chang et al. \[2024\]](#bib.bib10).
- •
  Assumption [C.3](#A3.Thmtheorem3 "Assumption C.3 (Bounded likelihood of 𝒟_off under 𝜋^0). ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
  is necessary to ensure that the KL between the prefix generating
  policy (empirical distribution over $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$)
  and the base policy $`\pi^{0}`$ is finite. If the size of the dataset
  $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}\rightarrow\infty`$ and the samples in
  $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ are all drawn IID from a policy
  $`\nu\in\Pi`$, then this assumption requires that cross-entropy
  between $`\nu`$ and $`\pi^{0}`$ is finite.

###### Assumption C.1 (Realizability of $`Q`$-function class).

There is a finite $`Q`$-function class
$`\mathcal{F}\subseteq\{f:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow[0,1]`$,
and that $`Q`$-function induced by any policy is realized in this class,
i.e., $`Q^{\pi}\in\mathcal{F}\;\forall\pi\in\Pi`$.

###### Assumption C.2 (Correctness and realizability of $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$).

We say that $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ is correct if it contains a
single correct trace $`\mathbf{y}`$ for every
$`\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}`$ and realizable if $`\exists`$ some policy
$`\mu\in\Pi`$ such that
$`{\mu}(\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{x})=1,\;\forall(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$.

###### Assumption C.3 (Bounded likelihood of $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ under $`\pi^{0}`$).

The KL divergence between base LLM $`\pi^{0}`$ and the policy
$`\mu\in\Pi`$ that perfectly fits the data is
$`\mathrm{KL}(\mu||\pi^{0})<\infty`$. In other words, this assumes that
the samples in $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ have a bounded likelihood
under the base LLM $`\pi^{0}`$, i.e.,
$`\mathrm{KL}(\mu||\pi^{0})=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}|}\sum_{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}})\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}}-{\log\pi^{0}(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}}\mid\mathbf{x})}<\infty`$.

###### Proof.

We prove the guarantee against the comparator policy $`\mu`$ from
Assumption [C.2](#A3.Thmtheorem2 "Assumption C.2 (Correctness and realizability of 𝒟_off). ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes").
Since $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ is correct and realizable by
$`\mu`$ (i.e., for each $`\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}`$ the unique correct
trace $`\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}}`$ satisfies
$`\mu(\mathbf{y}^{\mathbf{x}}\mid\mathbf{x})=1`$), we have
$`J(\mu)=J^{\star}`$. Thus it suffices to upper bound:

|     |                                            |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\bar{\pi}_{T}).`$ |     | (C.15) |

State distribution induced by $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. Let
$`\mathbf{s}\in\mathcal{S}`$ denote an autoregressive prefix-state.
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
samples prefix-states by drawing $`(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})`$ uniformly
from $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ and then sampling a prefix of
$`\mathbf{y}`$ (according to the algorithm’s prefix-selection rule).
Because $`\mu`$ deterministically generates the same trace
$`\mathbf{y}`$ in $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ for each
$`\mathbf{x}`$, this state distribution coincides with the
state-visitation distribution of $`\mu`$; we denote it by
$`d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}`$.

|     |                                                                                |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}\;\equiv\;d^{\mathrm{off}}_{\mathbf{s}}.`$ |     | (C.16) |

Let $`\pi^{0}`$ be the base LLM and $`\{\pi^{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}`$ be the
iterates produced by NPG / mirror descent with stepsize $`\eta`$ and
critic $`\widehat{Q}^{\,t}`$, and define the averaged policy:

|     |                                                                       |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\bar{\pi}_{T}\;:=\;\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi^{t}.`$ |     | (C.17) |

Performance difference lemma under $`d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}`$. Applying
performance difference
Lemma [C.4](#A3.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma C.4 (Performance difference lemma; [ , ]). ‣ C.4 Auxiliary Lemmas ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
with $`(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=(\mu,\pi^{t})`$ yields:

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                            |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\pi^{t})\;=\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{a}\sim\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})}\bigl[A^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})\bigr].`$ |     | (C.18) |

Using
$`A^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})=Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})-V^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s})`$
and the identity
$`\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{a}\sim\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})}[A^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})]=0`$,
([C.18](#A3.E18 "Equation C.18 ‣ Proof. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
can be rewritten as

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\pi^{t})\;=\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\bigl\langle Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})-\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\bigr\rangle\Bigr].`$ |     | (C.19) |

#### C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error.

Fix an iteration $`t`$. The critic is fit by least squares over a finite
class $`\mathcal{F}`$
(Assumption [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption C.1 (Realizability of 𝑄-function class). ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
using $`N`$ i.i.d. samples $`(\mathbf{s}_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k},z_{k})`$
where $`\mathbf{s}_{k}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}`$,
$`\mathbf{a}_{k}\sim\rho^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{k})`$ (see discussion
below on $`\rho^{t}`$), and $`z_{k}`$ is an unbiased target for
$`Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s}_{k},\mathbf{a}_{k})`$. Because rewards are
_terminal and binary_ in $`\{0,1\}`$, we have

|     |                                                                                                                              |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle 0\leq Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})\leq 1,\qquad 0\leq\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})\leq 1,`$ |     | (C.20) |

so we may take $`R=1`$ in
Lemma [C.6](#A3.Thmtheorem6 "Lemma C.6 (Lemma 15 in [ ] ). ‣ C.4 Auxiliary Lemmas ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes").
Therefore, setting $`\delta_{t}:=\delta/(2T)`$ and applying
Lemma [C.6](#A3.Thmtheorem6 "Lemma C.6 (Lemma 15 in [ ] ). ‣ C.4 Auxiliary Lemmas ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
with $`\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{F}`$, with probability at least
$`1-\delta_{t}`$,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |             |                                                          |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}},\,\mathbf{a}\sim\rho^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})}\Bigl[\bigl(\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})-Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})\bigr)^{2}\Bigr]\;\leq\;\frac{256}{N}\log\Bigl(\frac{2 | \mathcal{F} | }{\delta\_{t}}\Bigr)\;=\;\frac{256}{N}\log\Bigl(\frac{4T | \mathcal{F} | }{\delta}\Bigr).`$ |     | (C.21) |

Behavior distribution $`\rho^{t}`$ and pointwise domination. At
iteration $`t`$,
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
forms critic data by first sampling
$`(\mathbf{s}_{h},a_{h}^{\mathrm{off}})`$ uniformly from
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ and then sampling

|                                                                                  |                                                                                         |        |     |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --- |
|                                                                                  | $`\displaystyle a*{h}\;=\;\begin{cases}a*{h}^{\mathrm{off}}&\text{w.p. }\tfrac{1}{2},\\ |
| a*{h}\sim\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}*{h})&\text{w.p. }\tfrac{1}{2}.\end{cases}`$ |                                                                                         | (C.22) |

Let $`\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{h})`$ denote the (deterministic)
conditional action distribution induced by
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, i.e.,

|     |                                                                                      |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mu(a\mid\mathbf{s}_{h})\;:=\;\mathbf{1}\{a=a_{h}^{\mathrm{off}}\}.`$ |     | (C.23) |

Then the induced action-sampling (behavior) distribution used for critic
fitting is the mixture

|     |                                                                                                                                                      |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\rho^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{h})\;:=\;\frac{1}{2}\,\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{h})\;+\;\frac{1}{2}\,\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}_{h}).`$ |     | (C.24) |

Consequently, for every state $`\mathbf{s}`$ and action $`a`$, we have
the pointwise lower bounds

|     |                                                                                                                                                               |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\rho^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s})\;\geq\;\frac{1}{2}\mu(a\mid\mathbf{s}),\qquad\rho^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s})\;\geq\;\frac{1}{2}\pi^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s}),`$ |     | (C.25) |

and hence the pointwise domination inequalities

|     |                                                                                                                                               |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mu(a\mid\mathbf{s})\;\leq\;2\,\rho^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s}),\qquad\pi^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s})\;\leq\;2\,\rho^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s}).`$ |     | (C.26) |

In particular,
([C.25](#A3.E25 "Equation C.25 ‣ C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
also implies absolute continuity: if $`\rho^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s})=0`$
then $`\mu(a\mid\mathbf{s})=\pi^{t}(a\mid\mathbf{s})=0`$, so
$`\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\ll\rho^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$ and
$`\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\ll\rho^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$.

Then Cauchy–Schwarz and Jensen applied to
([C.21](#A3.E21 "Equation C.21 ‣ C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
yield, for $`\pi\in\{\mu,\pi^{t}\}`$,

|     |                      |                                                                                                                                                                                       |                                                                                                                |
| --- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\Bigl | \mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}},\,\mathbf{a}\sim\pi(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})}\bigl[\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})-Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{a})\bigr]\Bigr | \;\leq\;\epsilon*{\mathrm{crt}},\qquad\epsilon*{\mathrm{crt}}:=16\sqrt{2}\,\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\Bigl(\frac{4T | \mathcal{F} | }{\delta}\Bigr)}.`$ |     | (C.27) |

Taking a union bound over $`t\in[T]`$, with probability at least
$`1-\delta/2`$,
([C.27](#A3.E27 "Equation C.27 ‣ C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
holds for all $`t`$.

#### C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error.

The mirror-descent update at state $`\mathbf{s}`$ is the KL-regularized
maximization

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |     |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- |
|     | $`\displaystyle\pi^{t+1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})=\arg\max_{p(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})}\Bigl\{\eta\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),p(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\rangle-\mathrm{KL}(p(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\|\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}))\Bigr\}.`$ |     |

The first-order optimality condition implies that for any
$`p(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$,

|     |                                                                                                                                                        |                                                       |     |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\Bigl\langle-\eta\,\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot)+\nabla\_{r}\mathrm{KL}(r(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\|\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s}))\big | \_{r=\pi^{t+1}},\ p-\pi^{t+1}\Bigr\rangle\;\geq\;0.`$ |     | (C.28) |

Set $`p=\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$ and rearrange
([C.28](#A3.E28 "Equation C.28 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
to obtain

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                    |                                                |     |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\eta\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})-\pi^{t+1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\bigr\rangle\;\leq\;\Bigl\langle\nabla\_{r}\mathrm{KL}(r\|\pi^{t})\big | \_{r=\pi^{t+1}},\ \mu-\pi^{t+1}\Bigr\rangle.`$ |     | (C.29) |

Apply the KL three-point identity
(Lemma ([C.62](#A3.E62 "Equation C.62 ‣ Lemma C.5 (Three-point identity for KL). ‣ C.4 Auxiliary Lemmas ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")))
with $`p=\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$,
$`r=\pi^{t+1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$, and
$`q=\pi^{t}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})`$ to rewrite the right-hand side:

|     |                                                                   |                                                                                                                                      |     |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\Bigl\langle\nabla\_{r}\mathrm{KL}(r\|\pi^{t})\big | \_{r=\pi^{t+1}},\ \mu-\pi^{t+1}\Bigr\rangle=\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{t})-\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{t+1})-\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{t+1}\|\pi^{t}).`$ |     | (C.30) |

Combining
([C.29](#A3.E29 "Equation C.29 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
and
([C.30](#A3.E30 "Equation C.30 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
gives

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\eta\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu-\pi^{t+1}\bigr\rangle\;\leq\;\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{t})-\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{t+1})-\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{t+1}\|\pi^{t}).`$ |     | (C.31) |

Using
([C.20](#A3.E20 "Equation C.20 ‣ C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
and Pinsker’s inequality, we bound the shift term

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |     |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\eta\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\pi^{t+1}-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\;\leq\;\eta\,\|\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot)\|_{\infty}\,\|\pi^{t+1}-\pi^{t}\|_{1}\;\leq\;\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}+\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{t+1}\|\pi^{t}),`$ |     | (C.32) |

where the last inequality uses $`\|\widehat{Q}^{\,t}\|_{\infty}\leq 1`$
and
$`\|\pi^{t+1}-\pi^{t}\|_{1}^{2}\leq 2\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{t+1}\|\pi^{t})`$.
Adding
([C.31](#A3.E31 "Equation C.31 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
and
([C.32](#A3.E32 "Equation C.32 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
cancels $`\mathrm{KL}(\pi^{t+1}\|\pi^{t})`$ and yields

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                 |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\eta\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\;\leq\;\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{t})-\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{t+1})+\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}.`$ |     | (C.33) |

Taking expectation over $`\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}`$ and
summing over $`t=1,\dots,T`$ gives

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr]\;\leq\;\frac{D_{1}}{\eta T}+\frac{\eta}{2},`$ |     | (C.34) |

where

|     |                                                                                                                                                                             |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle D_{1}\;:=\;\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\mathrm{KL}\bigl(\mu(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\,\|\,\pi^{1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{s})\bigr)\Bigr].`$ |     | (C.35) |

Using $`\pi^{1}=\pi^{0}`$ and the definition of
$`\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{0})`$ under the
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$-induced state distribution
(Assumption [C.3](#A3.Thmtheorem3 "Assumption C.3 (Bounded likelihood of 𝒟_off under 𝜋^0). ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
we identify

|     |                                                        |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle D_{1}\;=\;\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{0}).`$ |     | (C.36) |

#### C.2.3 Combining Critic Error and Optimization Error.

Starting from
([C.19](#A3.E19 "Equation C.19 ‣ Proof. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
add and subtract $`\widehat{Q}^{\,t}`$:

|     |                                     |                                                                                                                                                                                              |     |        |
| --- | ----------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\pi^{t})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr]`$                                      |     |        |
|     |                                     | $`\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\bigl\langle Q^{\pi^{t}}(\mathbf{s},\cdot)-\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr].`$ |     | (C.37) |

On the high-probability event where
([C.27](#A3.E27 "Equation C.27 ‣ C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
holds for both $`\pi=\mu`$ and $`\pi=\pi^{t}`$, the critic-error term is
bounded by

|     |                                                                                                                                                              |                                                                                                                    |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\bigl\langle Q^{\pi^{t}}-\widehat{Q}^{\,t},\,\mu-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr]\;\leq\;\Bigl | \mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}},\,\mathbf{a}\sim\mu}\bigl[\widehat{Q}^{\,t}-Q^{\pi^{t}}\bigr]\Bigr | +\Bigl | \mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}},\,\mathbf{a}\sim\pi^{t}}\bigl[\widehat{Q}^{\,t}-Q^{\pi^{t}}\bigr]\Bigr | \;\leq\;2\,\epsilon\_{\mathrm{crt}},`$ |     | (C.38) |

which introduces no extra factor of $`H`$.

Averaging
([C.37](#A3.Ex4 "In C.2.3 Combining Critic Error and Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
over $`t=1,\dots,T`$ and using
([C.17](#A3.E17 "Equation C.17 ‣ Proof. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
we obtain

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |     |        |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\bar{\pi}_{T})\;\leq\;\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{s}\sim d^{\mu}_{\mathbf{s}}}\Bigl[\bigl\langle\widehat{Q}^{\,t}(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\,\mu-\pi^{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr]\;+\;2\,\epsilon_{\mathrm{crt}}.`$ |     | (C.39) |

Applying
([C.34](#A3.E34 "Equation C.34 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
to the first term in
([C.39](#A3.E39 "Equation C.39 ‣ C.2.3 Combining Critic Error and Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
yields

|     |                                                                                                                  |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\bar{\pi}_{T})\;\leq\;\frac{D_{1}}{\eta T}+\frac{\eta}{2}+2\,\epsilon_{\mathrm{crt}}.`$ |     | (C.40) |

Choose $`\eta:=\sqrt{\frac{2D_{1}}{T}}`$ to balance the first two terms
in
([C.40](#A3.E40 "Equation C.40 ‣ C.2.3 Combining Critic Error and Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
giving

|     |                                                                                   |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\frac{D_{1}}{\eta T}+\frac{\eta}{2}\;=\;\sqrt{\frac{2D_{1}}{T}}.`$ |     | (C.41) |

Combining
([C.36](#A3.E36 "Equation C.36 ‣ C.2.2 Mirror Ascent and NPG Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
([C.27](#A3.E27 "Equation C.27 ‣ C.2.1 Critic Estimation Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
and
([C.40](#A3.E40 "Equation C.40 ‣ C.2.3 Combining Critic Error and Optimization Error. ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")),
and recalling $`J(\mu)=J^{\star}`$, we conclude that with probability at
least $`1-\delta`$,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                          |             |                            |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | -------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\bar{\pi}_{T})\;=\;J^{\star}-J(\bar{\pi}_{T})\;\leq\;\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi^{0})}{T}}\;+\;\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\Bigl(\frac{T | \mathcal{F} | }{\delta}\Bigr)}\right).`$ |     | (C.42) |

∎

### C.3 Proof of Proposition [3.4](#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Worst-case separation with standard RL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")

Now, we prove our separation result in
Proposition [3.4](#S3.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 3.4 (Worst-case separation with standard RL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
that lower bounds the performance gap between standard RL and PrefixRL.
Here, standard RL runs
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
but now without any access to the off-policy dataset
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. In each iteration, the critic training
dataset $`\mathcal{D}_{t}`$ is now populated with $`(\mathbf{s},a,r)`$
tuples where both $`\mathbf{s}`$ and $`a`$ are sampled from the current
policy $`\pi^{t}`$. So, unlike PrefixRL we never sample the state or
prefix from $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. In this simple worst-case
instance we present below there is a single trajectory in
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ that is also what that the optimal policy
samples with probability $`1`$ and attains performance of
$`J(\pi^{\star})=1`$.

###### Proof.

We present (i) the MDP instance together with a choice of _base policy_
that generates the off-policy trace, and then (ii) an exponential lower
bound for standard on-policy RL without $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$,
and (iii) a horizon-independent (non-exponential) upper bound for
PrefixRL with $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$.

MDP instance (hidden rewarding binary string) and base policy. Fix a
horizon $`H`$ and an unknown binary string
$`\mathbf{b}=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{H})\in\{0,1\}^{H}`$. Let the state space
be

|     |                                                                |     |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\;=\;\{s_{0},s_{1},\ldots,s_{H}\},`$ |     | (C.43) |

where $`s_{h-1}`$ encodes the first $`h-1`$ actions taken so far
($`s_{0}`$ is the start state). The action space is
$`\mathcal{A}=\{0,1\}`$. Transitions are deterministic: from
$`s_{h-1}`$, taking action $`a_{h}\in\{0,1\}`$ moves to $`s_{h}`$. The
episode ends at $`s_{H}`$ with terminal reward

|     |                                                                                  |     |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle r\;=\;\mathbf{1}\{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{H})=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{H})\}.`$ |     | (C.44) |

Thus, exactly one length-$`H`$ action sequence earns reward $`1`$.

Let $`\pi^{\star}`$ be the deterministic policy that selects $`b_{h}`$
at $`s_{h-1}`$ for each $`h\in[H]`$. Then $`J(\pi^{\star})=1`$. For the
PrefixRL part, we also choose a _base policy_ $`\mu`$ and an off-policy
dataset $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$: we set $`\mu:=\pi^{\star}`$ and
let $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ contain the unique successful
trajectory of $`\mu`$, equivalently the state–action pairs

|     |                                                                              |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}\;=\;\{(s_{h-1},b_{h}):h\in[H]\}.`$ |     | (C.45) |

#### C.3.1 Exponential lower bound for standard on-policy RL (Algorithm 1 without $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$).

We analyze an on-policy variant of Algorithm 1 in which _there is no
off-policy dataset_: at each iteration $`t`$, the algorithm samples
$`N`$ full episodes only from its current policy $`\pi^{t}`$, observes
only terminal rewards $`r^{(t,i)}\in\{0,1\}`$, fits a critic, and
updates the policy. Let $`\widehat{\pi}_{T}`$ denote the (possibly
randomized) policy output after $`T`$ iterations (so the total number of
full episodes is $`TN`$). We prove that for any such algorithm, there
exists an instance $`\mathbf{b}`$ for which the expected suboptimality
gap is at least $`1-(TN+1)2^{-H}`$.

Yao’s minimax setup. By Yao’s minimax principle [Yao
\[1977\]](#bib.bib68), it suffices to fix an arbitrary adaptive
algorithm and analyze its expected performance when the instance is
random:

|     |                                                            |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{b}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{0,1\}^{H}),`$ |     | (C.46) |

and we write $`\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}},\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}`$ for
probability/expectation over this draw (and over the algorithm’s
internal randomness).

Per-rollout success probability is $`2^{-H}`$. Fix any rollout index
$`(t,i)`$. Condition on the full interaction history up to this rollout
and on the algorithm’s internal randomness. Under this conditioning, the
action string $`\mathbf{a}^{(t,i)}\in\{0,1\}^{H}`$ is some random
element (with an arbitrary distribution induced by the algorithm), while
$`\mathbf{b}`$ remains uniform and independent. Therefore,

|     |                                                                                      |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[r^{(t,i)}=1\mid\text{history}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\mathbf{a}^{(t,i)}=\mathbf{b}\mid\text{history}\right]=\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\{0,1\}^{H}}\mathbb{P}\!\left[\mathbf{a}^{(t,i)}=\mathbf{a}\mid\text{history}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}[\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{a}]=2^{-H}.`$ |     | (C.47) |

Taking expectation over the history yields the unconditional version:

|     |                                                                   |     |        |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}[r^{(t,i)}=1]\;=\;2^{-H}.`$ |     | (C.48) |

Probability of ever seeing a reward-1 rollout. There are exactly $`TN`$
rollouts in total. By a union bound and
([C.48](#A3.E48 "Equation C.48 ‣ C.3.1 Exponential lower bound for standard on-policy RL (Algorithm 1 without 𝒟_off). ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
we get the following upper bound on the probability of seeing a reward
$`1`$ rollout across all $`T`$ steps,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                             |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\exists\,t\leq T,\ i\leq N:\ r^{(t,i)}=1\right]\;\leq\;\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}[r^{(t,i)}=1]\;=\;TN\cdot 2^{-H}.`$ |     | (C.49) |

Bound the expected value of the returned policy. Let
$`\mathbf{a}\sim\widehat{\pi}_{T}`$ denote the length-$`H`$ string
generated by rolling out $`\widehat{\pi}_{T}`$ from $`s_{0}`$. On
instance $`\mathbf{b}`$,
$`J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})=\mathbb{P}[\mathbf{a}=\mathbf{b}]`$. We can
decompose this reward on two events: whether the training interaction
ever produced a reward-1 rollout or not:

|     |                                                                |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |     |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}[J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\exists\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=1\right]\cdot 1+\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\right]\cdot\sup_{\widehat{\pi}_{T}}\ \mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})\mid\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\right].`$ |     | (C.50) |

On the event $`\{\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\}`$, the algorithm has never
observed the rewarding string. Each zero-reward rollout rules out _at
most one_ candidate string, namely the realized action string
$`\mathbf{a}^{(t,i)}`$. Hence, conditioning on
$`\{\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\}`$ only implies that $`\mathbf{b}`$ is
not in a set of at most $`TN`$ excluded strings. Under the prior
$`\mathbf{b}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{0,1\}^{H})`$, the posterior is uniform
over the remaining candidates, so

|     |                                                                                                                                                                        |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{a}\in\{0,1\}^{H}}\ \mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{a}\mid\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\right]\;\leq\;\frac{1}{2^{H}-TN},`$ |     | (C.51) |

and therefore

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |     |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\sup_{\widehat{\pi}_{T}}\ \mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})\mid\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\right]\;=\;\sup_{\widehat{\pi}_{T}}\ \mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{a}=\mathbf{b}\mid\widehat{\pi}_{T},\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0)\right]\;\leq\;\frac{1}{2^{H}-TN}.`$ |     | (C.52) |

Substituting
([C.49](#A3.E49 "Equation C.49 ‣ C.3.1 Exponential lower bound for standard on-policy RL (Algorithm 1 without 𝒟_off). ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
and
([C.52](#A3.E52 "Equation C.52 ‣ C.3.1 Exponential lower bound for standard on-policy RL (Algorithm 1 without 𝒟_off). ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
into
([C.50](#A3.E50 "Equation C.50 ‣ C.3.1 Exponential lower bound for standard on-policy RL (Algorithm 1 without 𝒟_off). ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
yields

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}[J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})]\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\exists\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=1\right]\cdot 1+\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\forall\,t,i:\ r^{(t,i)}=0\right]\cdot\frac{1}{2^{H}-TN}\;\leq\;TN\cdot 2^{-H}+\frac{1}{2^{H}-TN}.`$ |     | (C.53) |

In particular, whenever $`TN\leq 2^{H-1}`$ we have
$`\frac{1}{2^{H}-TN}\leq 2^{-(H-1)}`$, and thus

|     |                                                                                                                            |     |        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}[J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})]\;\leq\;TN\cdot 2^{-H}+2^{-(H-1)}\;\leq\;(TN+2)\,2^{-(H-1)}.`$ |     | (C.54) |

Suboptimality lower bound and fix an instance. Since
$`J(\pi^{\star})=1`$, we obtain from
([C.53](#A3.E53 "Equation C.53 ‣ C.3.1 Exponential lower bound for standard on-policy RL (Algorithm 1 without 𝒟_off). ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
the gap bound

|     |                                                                                                            |     |        |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}[J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})]\;\geq\;1-(TN+2)\,2^{-(H-1)}.`$ |     | (C.55) |

By Yao’s minimax principle \[[Yao, 1977](#bib.bib68)\], there exists a
fixed hidden string $`\mathbf{b}`$ for which the same bound holds for
the algorithm on that instance. This proves the exponential lower bound
for standard on-policy RL.

#### C.3.2 Horizon-independent (non-exponential) upper bound for PrefixRL.

We now analyze
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
on the same instance _with_ $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$. As
discussed, the separation mechanism is that
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
explicitly samples states from $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, which are
precisely the prefix states along the $`\pi^{\star}`$ trajectory,
thereby forcing visitation of optimal-trajectory states.

Choosing $`\pi^{0}`$. Let $`\pi^{0}`$ denote the initialization policy
of
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"),
i.e. $`\pi^{1}=\pi^{0}`$. We choose $`\pi^{0}`$ to be the uniform policy
on $`\{0,1\}`$ at every state (crucially, $`\pi^{0}`$ is not instance
dependent):

|     |                                                                                                                   |     |        |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\pi_{0}(0\mid s)=\pi_{0}(1\mid s)=\tfrac{1}{2}\qquad\text{for all }s\in\{s_{0},\ldots,s_{H-1}\}.`$ |     | (C.56) |

Since $`\mu=\pi^{\star}`$ is deterministic on the states in
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, the KL term in Theorem 3.1 is not
exponential in $`H`$. Under the state-averaged convention used in
Theorem 3.1,

|     |                                                                                                                                                                 |     |        |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi_{0}):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^{\mu}_{s}}\Big[\mathrm{KL}\big(\mu(\cdot\mid s)\,\|\,\pi_{0}(\cdot\mid s)\big)\Big]\;=\;\log 2,`$ |     | (C.57) |

while under the summed convention it is $`H\log 2`$; in either case it
is not exponential in $`H`$.

Invoking PrefixRL guarantee in
Theorem [3.3](#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3.3 (Suboptimality gap of PrefixRL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")).
All assumptions required by
Theorem [3.3](#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3.3 (Suboptimality gap of PrefixRL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
hold on this instance with $`\mu=\pi^{\star}`$ and the corresponding
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}=\{\mathbf{b}\}`$. Therefore, applying
Theorem [3.3](#S3.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3.3 (Suboptimality gap of PrefixRL). ‣ 3.1 PrefixRL Objective-Consistency and Sample-Efficiency Guarantees ‣ 3 PrefixRL: On-Policy RL on Very Off-policy Prefixes ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
to
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes")
yields (with probability at least $`1-\delta`$) the bound

|     |                                                                                                                                                        |             |                            |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------- | -------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle J(\mu)-J(\bar{\pi}_{T})\;\leq\;\sqrt{\frac{2\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi_{0})}{T}}\;+\;\mathcal{O}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\!\Bigl(\frac{T | \mathcal{F} | }{\delta}\Bigr)}\right),`$ |     | (C.58) |

where $`\bar{\pi}_{T}`$ is the iterate-averaged policy output by
Algorithm 1. Since $`\mu=\pi^{\star}`$ on this instance,
$`J(\mu)=J(\pi^{\star})=1`$, and
([C.58](#A3.E58 "Equation C.58 ‣ C.3.2 Horizon-independent (non-exponential) upper bound for PrefixRL. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
implies a non-exponential suboptimality bound for PrefixRL. In
particular, with the choice
([C.56](#A3.E56 "Equation C.56 ‣ C.3.2 Horizon-independent (non-exponential) upper bound for PrefixRL. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
we have $`\mathrm{KL}(\mu\|\pi_{0})=\log 2`$ (or $`H\log 2`$ under the
summed convention), so the bound has no $`2^{-H}`$ term.

Separation mechanism. The on-policy exponential lower bound arises
because, without $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, an algorithm only
observes nonzero reward if it guesses the entire length-$`H`$ string
correctly in a single episode. In contrast, Algorithm 1 with
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ repeatedly samples _states along the
$`\pi^{\star}`$ trajectory_ via $`\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$ and
trains at those states using the mixture distribution of
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ C.2 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes").
Overall, this yields a non-exponential sample complexity, establishing a
worst-case separation.

#### C.3.3 Worst-Case Separation Result Between Standard RL and PrefixRL.

The analysis in the above subsection can be viewed either for a fixed
hidden string $`\mathbf{b}`$, or under a randomized instance
distribution. In particular,
let$`\mathbf{b}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{0,1\}^{H})`$, and note that under
this randomization the off-policy dataset
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}(\mathbf{b})`$
also changes with $`\mathbf{b}`$ since it contains the unique successful
trajectory $`(s_{h-1},b_{h})_{h=1}^{H}`$.

Lower bound for standard on-policy RL under random $`\mathbf{b}`$. For
any on-policy algorithm that runs for $`T`$ iterations with $`N`$ full
episodes per iteration (so $`TN`$ total episodes) and does not have
access to $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}`$, the expected value of the
suboptimality gap satisfies

|     |                                                                                                                                      |     |        |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[J(\pi^{\star}_{\mathbf{b}})-J(\widehat{\pi}_{T})\right]\;\geq\;1-(TN+2)\,2^{-(H-1)},`$ |     | (C.59) |

where $`\pi^{\star}_{\mathbf{b}}`$ denotes the optimal policy for
instance $`\mathbf{b}`$ and $`TN\leq 2^{H-1}`$.

Upper bound for PrefixRL under random $`\mathbf{b}`$. Now consider
Algorithm 1 (PrefixRL) run on the same randomized instance, where
$`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}(\mathbf{b})`$ is
provided to the algorithm. Choose the initialization $`\pi_{0}`$ to be
the uniform policy (so that $`\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\mathbf{b}}\|\pi_{0})`$
is not exponential in $`H`$), with
$`\mu_{\mathbf{b}}:=\pi^{\star}_{\mathbf{b}}`$ as the base policy that
generates $`\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{off}}(\mathbf{b})`$. Invoking the
previously proved Theorem 3.1 yields (with probability at least
$`1-\delta`$ over the algorithm’s sampling)

|     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |             |                            |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | -------------------------- | --- | ------ |
|     | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[J(\pi^{\star}_{\mathbf{b}})-J(\bar{\pi}_{T})\right]\;\leq\;\sqrt{\frac{2\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}\!\left[\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\mathbf{b}}\|\pi_{0})\right]}{T}}\;+\;\mathcal{O}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\log\!\Bigl(\frac{T | \mathcal{F} | }{\delta}\Bigr)}\right),`$ |     | (C.60) |

and for the uniform initialization $`\pi_{0}`$ we have
$`\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{b}}[\mathrm{KL}(\mu_{\mathbf{b}}\|\pi_{0})]=\log 2`$
under the state-averaged convention (or $`H\log 2`$ under the summed
convention), which is not exponential in $`H`$.

Applying Yao’s minimax principle. Equations
([C.59](#A3.E59 "Equation C.59 ‣ C.3.3 Worst-Case Separation Result Between Standard RL and PrefixRL. ‣ C.3 Proof of Proposition ‣ Appendix C Omitted Proofs ‣ Reuse your FLOPs: Scaling RL on Hard Problems by Conditioning on Very Off-Policy Prefixes"))
and
